vities (Prevention) Act, 1987 and any other osencefs),

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 26th May, 1993 

S.O. 1331.—In exercise of the powers conferred by sub¬ 
section (U of Section 5, read with Section 6 of the Delhi 
Special Police Establishment Act, 1946 (Act No. 25 of 1946). 
the Central Government, with the consent of the Government 
?[* Ciujara, vine Government of Gujarat, Home Department 
Notification No. CG/93/48/SB1ETDA] 169311918 dated 
19-4-1993, hereby extends the powers and jurisdiction of the 
members of the Delhi Special Police Establishment to the 
whole of the State of Gujarat for investigation of the offence# 
punishable under the sections 120-B IPC read with section 25 
Arms Act, Section 3 and 12 of Explosive Substances Act and 
Sections 3, 4 and 5 of Terrorist and Disruptive Activities 
(Prevention) Act, 1987 and any other offencefs), attempts, 
abetments and conspiracy in relation to or in connection with 
the said clfenccl s) committed in the course of the same 
transaction or arising out of the same facts in regard to the 
case II CR. No. 144/92 registered as PS Sami, Distt. 
Mehsana, (Gujarat) on 29lh October, 1992. 

[No. 228/38/93-AVD. IIJ

MINISTRY OF COAL 
Now Delhi, the 1st June, 1993 

S. O. 1344 :—Whereas by the notification of the Government of India in the Ministry of Coal 
number S.O. 90, dated the 24th December, 1991, issued under sub-soction (1) of section 7 of the Coal Bearing 
Areas (Acquisition and Development) Act, 1957 (20 of 1957) (hereinafter referred to as ihe said Act) and 
published in Part TI, Section 3, Sub-Section (ii) cf the Gazette of India, dated the 1 Ilia January, 1992, read with 
notification number S.O. 181, dated 17th December, 1992, published in Part 1J, Section 3, Sub-Soction (ii) of 
the Gazette of India, dated tlio 30th January, 1993 at pages 235-236, tbo Central Government gave notice of 
its intention to acquire the mining rights in the lands meat,uring 1831.40 acres (approximately) or 741.16 
hectares (approximately) in the locality specified in the Schedule appended to that notification; 

And whereas the competent authority, in pursuance of section 8 of the said Act, has made his report to 
the Central Government; 

And whereas the Central Government, after considering the report aforesaid and after consulting the 
Government of Madhya Pradesh, is satisfied that the mining rights- in the land measuring 1806.070 acres 
(approximately) or 731.160 hectares (approximately) described in the Schedule appended hereto should be acquired. 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers confeired by sub-section (1) of section 9 of the said Act, the 
Central Government hereby declares that the mining rights in the lands measuring 1806.70 acres (approxi¬ 
mately) or 731 .160 hectares (approximately) described in the said Schedule appondod hereto, are hereby 
acquired. 

The plan bearing number SECL/BSP/GM (Planning)/Land/I04 dated the 24th April, 1992 of the area 
covered by this notification may be inspected in the Office of ihe Collector, Raigarh (Madhya Pradesh) or in 
the Office ol the Coal Controller, 1, Council House Street, Calcutta or in the Office of the South Eastern 
Coalfields Limited (Revenue Section), Seopat Road, Bilaspur—495001 (Madhya Pradesh),

Act, the Central Government hereby gives notice of 
its intention to acquire the rights to mine, quarry, 
bore, dig and search for win, work and carry away 
minerals in the lands measuring 333.161 hectares 
(approximately) 'or 823.24 acres (approximately) 
described in the Schedule appended hereto. 

Note 1: The plans bearing number SECL/BSP/ 
GM (Planning)/Land/116 dated the 22nd September, 
1992 of the area covered by this notification may be 
inspected in the office of the Collector, Sahdol 
(Madhya Pradesh) or in the office of the Coal 
Controller, 1 Council House Street, Calcutta, or in 
the office of the South Eastern Coalfields Limited 
(Revenue Section) Seepat Road, Bilaspur-495001 
(Madhya Pradesh). 

Note 2. Attention is hereby invited to the provisions 
of section 8 of the aforesaid Act, which provides as 
follows :— 

Objection to acquisition : 

8.(1) Any person interested in any land in respect 
of which a notification under section 7 has been 


New Delhi, the 3rd June, 1993 

S.O. 1345 -Whereas by the notification of 
the Government of India in the then Ministry of 
Energy, Department of Coal, number S.O. 1767 
dated the 31st May, 1991 under sub-section (1) of 
section 4 of the Coal Bearing Areas (Acquisition and 
Development) Act 1957 (20 of 1957) (hereinafter 
referred to as the said Act) and published in the 
Gazette of India in Part-ll, Section 3, Sub-Section (ii) 
dated the 22nd June, 1991, the Central Government 
gave notice of its intention to prospect for coal in 
581.50 hectares (approximately) or 1436.88 acres 
(approximately) of the lands in locality specified in 
the Schedule annexed to that notification; 

And whereas the Central Government is satisfied 
that coal is obtainable In a part of the said lands 
described in the Schedule appended to this notification; 

therefore, in exercise of the powers con¬ 
ferred by sub-section (1) of section 7 of the said 


*)P?T 'PI ■II'SI'OT : ^5 . <J, ".9, 1 9'6 


'849 


[•STTIT II"-ratT 3 (ii )] 


issued may, within thirty days of the issue of the 
notification, object to the acquisition of the whole 
or any part of the land or of any rights in or over 
such land. 

Explanation: 

It shall not be an objection within the meaning of 
this section for any person to say that he himself 
desires to undertake mining operations in the land 
for the production of coal and that such operations 
should not be undertaken by the Central Government 
or by any other person. 

(2) Every objectionfunder sub-section (1) shall be 
made to the competent authority in writing and the 
competent authority shall give the objector an oppor¬ 
tunity of being heard either in person or by a legal 
practitioner and shall, after hearing all such objections 
and after making such further enquiry, if any, as he 
t hin ks necessary, either make a report in respect of 
the land which has been notified under sub-section

AWARD PART-I 

The Central Government by their Order No. L-22012/ 
147/91-IR(C.H) dated 27-6-1991 have referred the following 
Industrial Disputes to this Tribunal for adjudication under 
Section 10(J)(d) of the Industrial Disputes Act: 

“Whether (ho dismissal order from 6-5-1990 issued by 
the Sub Area Manager, W. C, Ltd., Chanda Rayat¬ 
wari Colliery, Chandrapur against Shri Yelkapalli 
Pcrmayya, Loader, is legal and justified ? If not, 
to what relief the workman is entitled ?” 

2. The Original Statement of Claim has been filed in 
Marathi at Ex. 2 on behalf of the workman Shri Yelkapalli 
Pcrmayya. by the leprescntativc of his union, while the copy 
of the Statement of Claim in English is at Ex. 2A. The case 
of the said workman, in short, is thus : 

The said workman was working as a permanent loader 
ar Chanda Rayatwari Colliery, Chandrapur, since 
26-10-974. In the Western Coal field there aro 
many collieries and one of them is Chanda Ruyyaf- 
witn Colliery. There are five to ztx irude unions in 
the said region, and Lad Bawta Koyla Knmgar 
Union, Chandrapur, is one of those unions which 
is a registered union, The abovesaid workman Shri 
Y. Pcrmayya was elected as a Branch President of 
the said union at Chanda Rayyatwari Colliery, and 
the management was informed about it. At the 
place of W'ork the management is required to give 
work to the permanent loader when he enters into 
the mine. In case he is not given any work by the 
company after he enters into the mino, he must get 
some amount of wages. It is not just and ptoper 
to ask the workmen to sit outside the place of 
work without any work being given to them. How¬ 
ever certain 18 loaders were simply asked to sit 
at the place of the work without any woik being 
given to them. After going out of the mine, those 
loaders went to the said workman Mr. Y. Permayya. 
the Branch Piesidcnt of the union, and infoimed 
him about the incident. The said workman then 
discussed the matter with the Deputy Manager Shri 
Sliastn. Those 18 loaders of Ihe second shift stayed 
lit the place of work in (he mine on 5-9-1989 think¬ 
ing that they would be given work at least during 
the third shift by the management. However no 
work was given to them even during the third shift. 
Hence the said workman, i.e. tha Branch President 
informed the superior officer about that incident 
>n the morning of 6-9-1989. The General Secretary 
realised the plot of the management and informed 
the Superintendent of the mine that they were com¬ 
pelling the workers to go on strike. Those 18 loaders 


wcie then issued the charge-shoots by the manage¬ 
ment. The chargesheets were issued against some 
oiher 5 workmen also. 

3. Thereafter the management issued a cliargcsheel dated 
12 - 9 - 1989 against the said workman Shri Y. Permayya. the 
Ur.,ri.li President of the Union, with a view to victimise him 
oil ihe ground of his union activities. This chargesheet was 
issued alter a period of seven days, and as such during that 
period the management was thinking of dismissing him from 
services. The enquiry officer was appointed to conduct an 
enquiry against him. The said workman denied the charges 
IcveBcd against him, On 12-10-1989 the said workman pre¬ 
sented tin application before the enquiry officer giving the 
details of his co-workers. However the enquiry officer refused 
to accept it and as such he had acted os an enquiry officer 
us well as a Public Prosecutor. Thereafter the said charge- 
sheeted v.cnkmen made an application for the change of the 
Enquiry Officer as he was not an impartial person. However 
his requests was not considered by the management. There¬ 
after an Additional charge-sheet was issued against that work¬ 
man and finally the management dismissed that workman 
from services. The enquiry conducted against him was not 
conducted properly and as per the rules of natural justice. 
He was dismissed from services on the ground of his union 
nervine*. The union, therefore, lastly prayed that this Tri¬ 
bunal should hold tlie action of the management as unjust, 
improper, nnd illegal and should direct the management to 
reinstate the said workman in services w.e.f. 6-5-1990, i.e. 
the date of the dismissal with full back wages and continuity 
of service. 

4. The management of M/s. Western Coal fields Ltd. 
(Rayatwari Sub Area by their Written Statement (Ex. 3) 
opposed the said claim of the union, and in substance, con¬ 
tended thus : 

The said workman Shri Yelkapalli Perniayyi was emp¬ 
loyed as a loader at Chanda Rvyatwari Colliery prior 
to his dismissal from srrvices, The charge sheet 
dated 12-9-1989 was issued (o him by the manage¬ 
ment. The charge against the wo.kmnn were 
thus :— 

"He instigated the workmen to go on illegal strike, in- 
tcifercd in the working of (he mines withoul the 
petnusaion of the competent authority and gave 
threats to the executives (officer's) of the mine, 
which acts resulted in loss of production to the 
management. The abovesuid a eft amounted mis¬ 
conduct under the Standing Orders No. 1 ?TB) (9). 
( 16 ). nod (34) of the Certified Standing orders as 
applicable to Chanda Rayatwari Co’iiei y and its 
workmen”.

The Issues framed at Ex. 4 are : 

(11 Whether the enquirv held against the workman in

(2) Whether the dismissal order from 6-5-1090 issued

(4) What Award? 

R. Tssuc No. 1 vi/. "Whether the enquiry h't.i against ffie

AWARD 

The Government of India as per Order No. E-12012/214/ 
9-IRB.II dated 22-11-1991 have referred this industrial dispute 

to this Tribunal for adjudicating the following issue . 

“Whether the action of the Management of Indian Bank 
in not granting daftarv allowance to Sri. G. Ravecn- 
dran. Sub-staff, Attinpnl Branch on his rc-instatement 
in service is justified 7 If not, to what relief the said 
workman is entitled to 7” 

2, In pursuance to notices issued from this Tribunal both 
sides entered appearance and filed statements advancing their 
respective contentions Thereafter the case was adjourned for 
evidence and posted to 29-4-199). On that day, when the case 
was taken up, the general secretary of the union has filed a 
memo stating that the claim u n der this reference is not pres¬ 
sed. The learned counsel for the management also submitted 
(hat the management has no objection in closing the matter 
as not pressed. 

7. In view what is stated above, the case is closed as not 
pressed and an award is passed accordingly.

This dispute coming on for final hearing on Tuesday, the

and upon bearing the arguments of ThiruvaJerguJ G. R. Ed¬ 
mund and A. D. Jagdishchandran Advocates appearing for the

AWARD 

This dispute between the workman and the management of 
tinned Commercial Bank, Zonal Office, Madras-1 arises out 
of a reference under section 10(1)(d) of the Industrial Dis¬ 
putes Act, 1947 bv the Government of India in its Order 
No. I.-12012/27()/90-TR-B/ll) dated 26-12-1990 of the Minis¬ 
try of 1 itbonr for adjudicrrioii of the following issue : 

“Whether the action of the Management of UCO Bank 
in dismissing Sh, A. Sappani, Watchman-cum-Peon 
from the services of the Dank is justified ? TC not to 
what relief the workman Is entitled V' 

2. The Petitioner-workman states as follows : 

He was working as vvatchman-cum-peon at the Branch 
of Ihe Respondent at Pcttni, Tirunelveli. For ■ ’rtain 
aliened acts of misconduct committed by him bet¬ 
ween I °79-1983 in respect of transactions at the said 
branch he was given a charge shed dt. 4-4-86. The 
matter was investigated also by Central Bureau of 
Investigation, Madras which gave n report lhat there 
were no materials to launch a criminal prosecution, 
But, acting on the said report, the respondent has 
issued the charge sheet to which the petitioner gave 
his explanation. Then a domestic enquiry was ordered 
and conducted. The petitioner has no knowledge of 
English, in which the Domestic Enquiry Proceedings 



qjTft V TjWftl: 19, 109:>/wtac2A, 13) 1,5 


Um IL--/T* 3f(ii)l 


were conducted. His request to engage a Lawyer was 
negatived. The Enquiry Officer gave a report dated 
19-10-88 holding that the charges have been proved, 

TO the socond show cause notice the petitioner gave 
his reply dt. 7-12-88. The Respondent passed an order 
dt. 13-12-8&, dismissing the petitioner from service. 

The Appellate authority dismissed petitioner's appeal 
also. Hence the petitioner started the conciliation 
proceedings which has-, also, failed, 

3. The. petitioner was not, given, a copy of. the C.B.l. report. 
Hence he was not able to make effective cross-examination of 
Management witnesses. Refusal to engage a lawyer has also 
adversely affected his defence. Important documents have 
been admitted in the evidence, without competent witnesses 
proving them. The enquiry officer acted in a biased manner. 

Tho fact that the petitioner who has studied only upto 7fh 
standard could not have committed the serious acts without 
paiticpataiou and connivance of other persons in the Bank, 
was not considered at all. The dismissal of the petitioner from 
service is totally unjustified. The findings of tne enquiry offi¬ 
cer are perverse. Hence an award may be passed, directing 
the respondent to re-instate the petitioner in service with all 
benefits. 

4. The Respondent states aa follows :— 

The Petitioner joined the post at Pettai branch, Tiruncl- 
vely, as a Peon-cum-watchman on 27-2-1974. He was 
given a tamll translation of the charge sheet at his 
request. The 4 charges against him are as follows : 

1. The charge sheeted employee utilised one Athankar- 
aiyan to force withdrawal slips for a total amount 
of Rs. 3000/- and drew the amount from S.B, A/c. 
No. 1952. 

2. He pledged his jewels in the name of Thim M. 
Subramaniatn and obtained jewel loan and discharged 
the same- by fraudulently withdrawing money from 

S.B, A ft. NO. 1952. 

3. He forged ihe signature of Thlrii Balasubramnrdan 
and withdrew money from S. U. Account No. 1952. 

‘1. I. He opened two fictitious S.B. Account Nos, 1458 
and 1952 respectively in the name of Ramachandran 
and Balasubramanian, 

2. lie deposited the proceeds of 22 instruments in the 
said f.rlilious accounts opened by him. 

5. A representative of the union conducted Hit defence. 

19 witnesses have been examined by t,he Management, Oral 
and documentary evidences have established the charges 
except the charge No. 4(1). The Domestic Enquiry officer 
has recorded his findings on sufficient evidence. The disci¬ 
plinary authority fins ’agreed with the report of the Domestic 
Enquiry Officer and gave a hearing to the petitioner regard¬ 
ing punishment. After considering the material!?, the 
disciplinary authority passed an order "dt, 13-12-88 dismissing 
Ihe petitioner from service. The appellate authority rightly' 
con finned the order and dismissed the appeal. The Res¬ 
pondent was justified in refusing engagement of a lawyer 
by the petitioner. His defence representative General Secre¬ 
tary of the union is a very competent person, Full oppor¬ 
tunities have been given to the petitioner. Report of the 
being a confidential document, could not have been - ' 
given to the petitioner. The domestic enquiry officer had 
no bias against the petitioner. The petitioner has enough 
knowledge and ability to handle the work of clearina 
cheques and doing other clerical jobs which are generally 
performed bv employees above the rank of a peon. The 

fairness of the domestic enquiry may be decided as a pre¬ 
liminary issue and if the same is found to be unfair, the 

respondent is willing to let in further evidence to prove 

the charges. The punishment of dismissal Is not dis¬ 
proportionate The claim is liable to be dkm'ssed. 

6. Point- for determination arc as follows :_ 

1 Whether the domestic enquiry has been conducted" 
f.ilrly and properly 7

available or are they perverse 7 

3. Whether the punishment is adequate and just 7 

7. Though the respondent wanted a preliminary enquiry 
in the first instance regarding the question of fairness of 
the enquiry both sides have not pressed the eame. On the 
other hand, the Respondent’s counsel has sta-ed that the 
I.D. may bo disposed off on merits without taking any 
further evidence and by using the evidentiary manffQl* 
already given at the domestic enquiry. Hence no oral evi¬ 
dence was given in this Tribunal. The documenU relating 
to tho domestic enquiry like the charge sheet, petitioner’s 
explanation, record of evidence, the domestice enquiry report 
and dismissal order have been marked as Exhibits Ml to Mil, 

8 . Point No. 1 : The petitioner is after all a peon watch¬ 
man vCth educational qualification of study upto 7lh class 
or standard. The major charge is charge No. 4 consisting 
of two parts. The first part is that the petitioner allegedly 
opened a ficlious S.B. A/c. No. 1458 on 23-4*89 in the name 
of V, Ramachandran and another fictions S.B. A|c. No. 
1952 on 11-12-89 in the name of Q. Balasubraman on. After 
opening these accounts, the petitioner is said to have 
deposited Ro. 1,11,030 being the proceeds of 12 instruments 
received at the Pettai Branch for collection towards out- 
station cheques or for payment across the counter, and th:n 
withdraw the amounts through withdrawal forms ,'lip3) !»nd 
that he also tempered with and destroyed he relevant bank 
records. Charge Nos. 1 to 3 are in suhstance ancillary 
charges relating to the different transactions in which tho 
petitioner fradulcntly withdrew the amounts from the above 
stated S.B. A|c.s. 

9, The stand of the respondent that the report of Thiru 
Ramarajan, Inspector of Police beloging to the CBI who 
conducted am investigation after registering the crime is a 
confidential one and that a copy thereof could not have 
been furnished to the petitioner before the commencement 
of tho domestic enquiry Is out untenable, The Re pondent 
has also failed to give copies of the reports of 2 departmental/ 
internal investigations conducted by Rnjagopal and Sund?ram, 
Having the magnitude of the charges in mind, tho reports 
of the CBI Inspector and also the 2 departmental in pec’ors 
are mo*5t vital documents, copies of which should hive b*en 
furnished to the petitioner to enable him to have a meaningful 
opportunity for cross examining the witnesses and defend ng 
himself. The respondents failure to furnish copies of tbe*se 
documents in my view has adversely affected the pe'it loner's 
capacity to defend himself and therefore I hold that the 
Domestic Enquiry has not been conducted in accordance 
with ihe principles of natural justice and hence it is unfair 
and vitiated. 

10. Point No,—-2 : Even assuming that the Domestic 
enquiry has been fairly conducted we may proceed to decide 
2nd and 3rd points in the absence of any further evidence 
given before us, by assessing and relying solely on the evi¬ 
dence recorded at the domestic enquiry. The delinquent 
Sappani is charged with a very serious crime of opening a 
ficlious S.B. A/c. No. 1952 on 11-12-89 in the name of one 
Mr, G. Balasubramanian and then crediting the said’ a/c. 
with monies beloging to other payees and finally withdrawing 
several thousands of rupees from the said S.13. A|c. by 
using forged withdrawal slips. The account was opened 
when tho Branch Manager M.W.l SrlmVasan was at the helm 
of the Branch at Pettai, Tirunelveli. The monies fradulently 
credited into S.B. A/c. represented the proceeds of collection 
cheques sent by the respondents branch at Madras, Sowcareet, 
clearance of all these cheque* was done at Tirunelveli 
Branch of Tamil Nadu Bank, as the respondents branch! 
bad no clearing facility! Material witnesses exam’ned at the 
domestic enauiry are M.W.l, the then Branch Manager 
Mr. V. Srinivr.’.an, M.W.2. Krinhnan, then clerk of the 
Branch. M.W.4 Ganapathy Subramanian, another clerk 
M.W.5 Muthukumaraswamy, a clerk cum typ'st, M.W.6 
Ramasamv then Asst. Manager, M.W.7 Sankarnfingain, 
another clerk, M.W.8 S. Balasundram also a Clerk M.W, 9 
Appsdurai, them cashier M.W, 15 Joseph George, another 
cashier and M.W.6 Ramskrlduton, th-’n Chief Cashier of

the Branch. Besides these Bank, officials, M.W.3 Dr. Ali 
handwriting expert attached to C.B.I. Madras M.W.13;

who made the departmental inspections ale M.W 14 
Rajagqpaian and M.W.18 Stmdaram. 

11 . Meet oi' die.® witnesses who have worked in the Pcttai 
Brahch of the Respondent have painted the petitioner as the 
ace-Viilain with great talents required of a clerk or Officer 
of the Bank and as one capable of doing duties which are 
far above the job responsibilities of a w'ateh man. Whose, 
duty is to have the keys of the outer most door of the 
office building to open and close the building, bring Tnpals 
and place them before the Branch Manager. M.W.2 
Krinhuan who is fl clerk, depired that the petitiiner Sappani 
was doing clerical works such as : 

"He had been issuing tokens, sorting cheques for clear¬ 
ing ho has even taken balancing work, he has written 
section day book; some times he has even debited 
tho accounts.”, 

M.W.. 15 Joseph George, speaking about the greater role 
played by the petitioner deposed an follows :— 

“He did balancing, loan ledgers willing supplementary 
book, helping clearing etc. 

Mr. Sappani used to help up while writing the clearing 
Register. The cheques will be taken out sepenttcly to pre¬ 
pare. bank schedules. 

At the paying in slip will be handed over to me to write 
the clearing register. By the time I finish writing the clear¬ 
ing register and tally it the bankwise schedule will be pre¬ 
pared. by Mr. Sappani and hand it over to mo. After that 
I will go for clearing. 

In the same vein, M.W.16 Ramakrishnau, Chief Cashier at 
the relevant time has told that the petitioner was virtually 
doing the job of a clerk. The deposition iuns as follows :— 

* He used to help staff members in all spheres of help includ¬ 
ing receiving cheques from customers, affixing the stamps, 
preparing bank-wise schedules, taking balancing and totalling, 
clearing scroll tallying. I have seen him writing day book 
also twice. According to M.W.4 Ganapalhy Subramanian, 
the items of processing work in relation to clearance of 
cheques are as follows :•— 

Generally the cheques are accompanied by deposit challans, 
schedu'es, from branches and OBC realisation schedules. The 
amounts mentioned in the schedule received from branches. 
The amount mentioned in t.he schedule received from bran¬ 
ches are recorded in the sundry column. The cheque parti¬ 
culars are recorded in the appropriate cOiumns. By cheques 
particulars I mean the bank branch, amount and cheque 
number. In case of OBC realisations, the amount mention¬ 
ed in the schedule received from other banks are recorded 
in bills, column. In respect of the cheques received from 
parties, the challan amount is entered in deposit column, 
and the particulars relating to cheques are entered in ap¬ 
propriate columns”. 

Such evidence has been advaced as proof of the facts that" 
the petitioner alone was responsible for making alterations 
in the cheques B3 forms withdrawal forms etc. to carry out 
the fraud. That these could not be swallowed as true is 
shown by the evidence MAV.J Muthukumaraswamy, then 
clerk. Ho says that the B3 forms drawn in favour of 
Sowcarpct Branch has been tampered with by writing word 
“returned" written by Ramakrishnan ; M.W.16) etc. and 
S. Rrishnan (M.W.2). 

12. The very way in which the fictions S,B, A,'c. 1952 of 
G. Balasubramanian wan opened has been spoken to by 
M.W.l Srmivaxan, the Branch Manager concerned, He ad¬ 
mits that he has not seen Balasubramanian, that he has 
simply authenticated the specimen signature cars of G. Bala- 
Subramanian' ar.<J that he did not know who introduced the 
customer or impostor Mr. Balasubramanian. He and other 
-Witnesses freely admitted th,*, they permitted opening of 
accounts without even seeing the face of the customers on 


1993/JYA13TH<V 29, 1915 [Part II—SBc, 3(ii)| 


whose behalf the Ales, wore opened and without screening 
the introduction. Eulogising the capac.ty of the petitioner 
M.W.l Mimvosan then Branch Manager, said that he work¬ 
ed as a clerk by writing scrolls, supplementary ledger, clear¬ 
ing register and B3 forms. He wcnl on to say that hio 
signatures on B3 forms have been forged by the Petitioner. 

The various registers and forms regarding collection pf 
outstation cheque's such as clearing register, clearing scroll, 
B.3 forms have to be filled up and. maintained only by 
clerks or officers and not n peon like ;ne petit oner. One 
must remembers that all the formalities regarding opening 
of accounts have to be observed only by the Branch Manager 
and not a member of the sub-staff. The evidence of the 
Bank employees reveals that more than tie petitioner Sappani, 
other Bank employees should have played their part in the 
fraud. 'Hie C.B.I, Inspector M.W.17 Ramaroj has opined 
that the materials were not strong enough to warrant tiling 
a charge sheet in a court of laws. He deposed that there 
arc nevertheless grounds necessitating a departmental en¬ 
quiry against Petitioner Sappani alone to the exclusion of 
Bank’s other officials. This view, in ihe back ground of 
evidence and nature of the crime, is quite far fetched. M.W. 3 
Dr. Ail haud writing expert attached to the CBI has com¬ 
pared SI to S31 specimen writings of the petit onir wilh hte 
questioned writings. The alleged specimen writings teken 
from Sappani by M.W.17 Rami.raJ, Inspector of Police have 
net been taken under ordeis of any court. Even assuming 
that the petitioner Sappani has actually written ccr ain crucial 
entries in B,3 forms, we cannot think for a moment that 
he had done it on his own without oral orders given by 
the officials of the Bank. M.W.15 Thiru Joseph George 
told that Thiru Sappani Petitioner was permitted to receive 
cadi from the counter of the Branch acting as a representa¬ 
tive of an account holder of the Branch who was employed 
in the State Bank of India, TirunclvcH as Cashier and that 
the witness made the payment on th© advice of then Cashier 
of the Branch who is M.W.16 Thiru V. Ramakri hnan. This 
is to be seen as the thin er.d of the wedge or tcheme in 
which the petitioner wig made to act as a tool by other 
officers. 

The evidence of M.W.10 Subnunanlan who took Jewel 
loan by pleading the jewels at the Pcttai Branch is that the 
jewels belonging to the petitioner were pledged by M.W. 10 
in as much as n jewel loan in the name cf the petitioner 
himself cannot be directly availed. This is not n misconduct 
affecting the bank in any manner. Due to mutual trust 
M.W.10 Subramanian could have posed as the owner of 
the jewels which really belonged to the petitioner and obtain¬ 
ed tlie loan for the petitioner’s benefit by going through the 
formalities. This witness Subramanian is a member of the 
sub-staff of another bank at Ttrunelvdi. After considera¬ 
tion of ihe voluminous oval evidences, I believe that the 
evidences are so contradictory and unconvincing that we 
cannot place any reliance on it to hold the petitioner guilty 
of charges, Unless the clerks and officers of the Bunk have 
themselves played their own parts such a fraud cannot be 
perpetrated right from the opening of S.B. A/c. No. 1952. 
in the name of O. Balasubramanian followed by diversion of 
the monies belonging to other payees to the said fictitious 
account and then withdrawal of huge amounts cm the 
strength of fradulent withrawnl slips. We cannot but feel 
that the petitioner is made a scapegoat for the serious wrongs 
committed by his superiors running the branch. I therefore 
opine that the charges against the peon-cum-watchman are 
not proved, that he has been a victim at the hands of the 
Officers, and (.hat the findings of the Domestic enquiry officer 
are nothing hut perverse. I therefore, find on point No 2 
that the charges are not proved and that the findings cannot 
be sustained. It follows that tho petitioner ennnot be 
awarded any punishment. However, in circumstances of tho 
case there must be some suffering inflicted on petitioner 
Sappani who could have allowed himself to be used as a 
Pawn in doinj: jobs which are far above his responsibilities 
Hence the petitioner shall be content with drawing 75% of 
back wages alone. The points arc answered accordingly. 

13. In the result, an award Is passed directing the Res¬ 
pondent to re-instate the petitioner in service' and provide 
him continuity of service ulongwltb attendant benefits.

ANNEXURE 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL

PRrSHNT : 

Justice Sbri R, G. Sindhakar, Presiding Officer.

PARTIES : 

Toe Employers in relation to the management of Bank 
ot Maharashtra, 

AND 

Tke.'r Workmen. 

APPEARANCES : 

for the Management—No appearance. 

F 01 the workmen—No appearance. 

INDUSTRY : Banking STATE : Maharashtra 

Bombay, the 13th May, 1993 
AWARD 

This is a icfcrencc made to this Tribunal by the Labour 
Department, Government of India, vide letter No, L-12012/ 
65/91-IR (B-II). for answering : 

“Whether the action of the Management of Bank of 
Maharashtra Nagpur for rejecting the claim of Cycle 
Allowance with effect from 1-1-1982 to Shrl U. C. 
Ramble: (Sub-staff) is justified 7 If not, to what 
relief is the workman entitled ?’* 

Notice was issued on 29-7-1991 to both the parties for 
a”,-caring before this Tribunal on 27-9-1991. None appeared 
on behalf of the Union and a fresh notice was served and yet 
no appearance was made on behalf of the Union and the 
matter was, adjourned for ex parte hearing on 5-2,1992, even 
on which date none on behalf of the Union appeared. 

In the ab.ence oE any statement of claim it is not possible 
to know the nature of dispute. There was none who could 
be questioned on the point. It Is therefore not possible to 
hold action of the management of Bank of Maharashtra as 
unjustified, and the workman is therefore not etitled to any 
relief. 

R. G. SINDHAKAR, Presiding Officer

hereby publishes the award of the Central Government

THE GAZETTE OE INDIA: JUNE 19, 1993/JYAISTHA 29, 1915 [Par- II -Sec. 3(ii; ; 


ANNEXURE 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL 
TRIBUNAL NO. 1, AT BOMBAY 

PRESENT : 

Justice Shri R. G. Sindhakar, Presiding Officer. 
Reference No. CGIT1/36 ot 1991 

PARTIES : 

Employers in relation to the Management of Bank of 
Maharashtra, 

AND 

Their Workmen. 

APPEARANCES : 

For the- Management—No appearance. 

For the workmen—No appearance. 

INDUSTRY : Banking STATE : Maharashtra 

Bombay, the 13th May, 1993 


AWARD 

This is a reference made to this Tribunal vide letter No. 
L-12012/464/90-IR (B-Il) dated 5th April 1991, by the 
Ministry of Labour. Government of India. 

The point to be answered is : 

“Whether the action of the Management of Bank of 
Maharashtra in imposing a punishment by stoppage 
of six annual increments on Shri K. D. Sonar 
Musalkar is justified If not, what relief the work¬ 
man is entitled to ?” 

On receipt of this reference, notices were issued to both 
the parties for appearing before this Tribunal on 14-5-1991. 
But no one appeared on behalf of the workman. 

. The Management, namely the Bank of Maharashtra in its 
statement dated 28-8-1992, it has been Contended that the 
workman has been penalised for having defrauded the Bank 
by withdrawing a sum of Rs. 500 by forging the signature 
of an account holder and he also committed other misconduct 
as detailed in the chargeshect. The Bank further stated that 
the workman had admitted the fact in the departmental 
enquiry conducted against him. It was further stated that 
no statement has been filed on behalf of the workman 
inspite of several opportunities given to him. 

It is seen from the Rojnama, maintained in this office, 
that the workman was represented by Mr. C. N. Kffikarni on 
26-6-1991 after receipt of the notice. The matter came to 
be adjourned for filing of the statement of claim bv the 
Union, but no statement came to be filed on the following 
dates also. Instead of filing the statement of claim adjourn¬ 
ment was sought. On 16-1-1992 none appeared on behalf 
of both the parties, and notice was issued to the Union for 
filing the statement of claim on 13-4-1992. Thereafter, 
notice for ex-parte hearing on 24-6-1992, and thereafter, 
on 9th July 1992, The matter came to be adjourned further 
as the Presiding Officer of this Tribunal was not appointed- 
It was then fixed on 27th April 1993, and no appearance was 
made on behalf of either of the parties. Under this circums¬ 
tances, in the absence of any material to show that the 
action of the managemet of Bank of Maharashtra in imposing 
tne punishment of stoppage of six annual increment as unjust, 
that It is not so. 

R. G. SINDHAKAR, Presiding Officer

New Delhi, the 26th May, 1993 

S O 1358.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publishes tho award of the Industrial Tribunal, Kollam 
as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between 
the employers in relation to the management of State Bank 
of Travancorei and their workmen, which was received by 
the Central Government on 25-5-1993. 

[No. L-12012/57/92-IR (B-Ill)l 
S. K. JAIN, Desk Officer 


ANNEXURE 

IN THE COURT OF THE INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL, 
KOLLAM 

(Dated, this the 13th day of May, 1993) 

PRESENT : 

Sri C. N. Sasidharan. Industrial Tribunal, 

IN 

Industrial Dispute No. 21/92 
BETWEEN 

The Managing Director, State Bank of Travancore, Head 
Office Poojappura, Trivandrum. 

AND 

Die General Secretary, Slate Bank of Travancore Staff 
Union, Central Office, P.B. No. 5601, Trivandrum. 

AWARD 

This industrial dispute has been referred for adjudication 
to this Tribunal by the Government of India as per order 
No. L-12012/57/92-IR (B-II1) dated 20-7-1992 for adjudicat¬ 
ing the following issue : 

“Whether the action on tho part of the management 
of State Bank of Tranvancore in denying the post 
of DCCP to Sri Mohankumar at the Peyad Branch 
of the bank is fair and justifiable 7 If not, to what 
relief the said workman is entitled to 7" 

2. The union representing the workman Sri Mohan Kumar 
has filed a claim statement and the contentions are briefly 
us below : The subordinate staff in the management Bank are 
entitled to special allowance for discharging certain specified 
duties as per the terms of service applicable to them. The 
post of Daftary-Cum-Cash Peon (‘DCCP’ for short) is one 
attracting special allowance. Every 1 branch of this Bank 
has DCCP. The post of DCCP at Peyad branch fell vacant on 
7-8-1989. The two peons working in that branch on that day 
wen on probation. Temporary employees and employees oh 
probation are not be entrusted with any duties attracting 
special allowance as per staff circular of the Bank 
dated 7-3-1992, The workman Sri .Mohan Kumar was con¬ 
firmed in the Bunks service with effect from 2B-1-1990 and he 
has been doing the duties of DCCP from the date. But the 
management did not pay him special allowance and the refusal 
of the management to confirm him as DCCP with effect from 
20-1-1990 is justifiable and illegal.. Sri Mohan Kumar whs 
initially posted to the Zonal Office of the Bank on 20-7-1989 
along with five others. He was finally posted to Peyad branch 
on 27-10-1989. Sri K. Surendran posted as peon joined at 
Peyad branch on 25-10-1989. The post of DCCP of that 
branch fell vacant on 7‘8'1989, Neither of the peons of 
that time war posted as DCCP and not paid special allowance 
as both of them were on probation. The Regional Manager 






[gm 3 (ii)] 


VI XfSMW . *[a 18, 1 89 J.jwfa 19)3 


1891 


on representation from Sri Mohan Kumar adviced the branch 
Manager that Sri Mohan Kumar is eligible for the post of 
DCCP from the' date of confirmation. Sri Mohan Kumar was 
the only confirmed peon at Peyad brunch as on 20-1-1990 who 
was entitled to the post of DCCP. The management confirmed 
Sri Surendran also on 30-8-1990 and it was communicated 
only on February 1992 i.e. during the pendency of this dis¬ 
pute, It is therefore illegal and liable to be nullified. The 
management in a similar case directed to pay special allow¬ 
ance to an employee who was transferred to Punnayurkulam 
branch of the Bank with effect from the date he joined there 
though there was already another peat! who enjoyed branch 
seniority, but was on probation. The denial of the post of 
DCCP to Sri Mohan Kumar amounts to discrimination. The 
action of nuutagaement in designating Sri Surendran as DCCP 
and payment of special allowance with effect from 10-2-1992 
during the pendency of this dispute is a violation of Sec. 33 
of the Industrial Disputes Act. The prayer in for confirma¬ 
tion of Sri Mohan Kumar as DCCP of Peyad branch with 
effect from 20-1-1990 continuously and ,for payment of 
special allowance. 

3. The management opposed the claim of the union and 
the contentions of management are briefly as under : 

Sri Damodaran who was working as peon in Peyad branch 
was receiving DCCP allowance till 23-11-1989 on 
which date he was transferred from .there, Sri K. 
Surendran newly recruited a* peon joined Peyad 
branch on 23-10-1989. Sri Mohan Kumar newly 
recruited as peon joined Trivandrum Zonal Office of 
the Bank on 20-7-1989 and he joined Peyad branch 
on 27-10-1989. Sri Mohan Kumar was confirmed 
with effect from 20-1-1990 as peon and Sri Surendran 
was confirmed vvith effect from 25-4-1990. So the ' 
claim of the union based on letter dated 30-8-1990 
stated to be of the Regional Manager was not given 
effect to and the management was only considering 
the eligibility of Sri Surendran also. to get the 
allowance. The decision to designate Sri Surendran 
as DCCP was taken on 19-10-1991 which was com¬ 
municated to the Deputy General Manager on 
13-1-1992. The letter of the Asst. Labour Com¬ 
missioner intimating his decision to discuss the 
dispute raised by the union was reccived on 17-11992 
and there is no violation of any practice of law. 
The normal practice followed in the Bank for filling 
up special allowance carrying posts is centerwise/ 
branch seniority. Seniority is normally counted with 
reference to the date of joining the branch and not 
based on the date of • confirmation. Confirmation of 
service alone is not the only criterion for deciding 
eligibility. Jn Punnayurkulam branch the two peons 
joined on the same day and seniority was counted 
based on the date of birth of the concerned emp¬ 
loyees. This has no connection with the present 
case. The service - condition of .Sri Mohan Kumar 
was mot altered during the pendency of conciliation 
proceedings. Sri Mohan Kumar is not entitled to 
the post of DCCP in Peyad branch as he was not 
senior in that branch. According to the manage¬ 
ment the 'Workman is not entitled to any relief. 

4. No 'evidence has been adduced by cither side. How¬ 
ever the union has produced six documents and the manage¬ 
ment has produced one document. I am markings six docu¬ 
ments ns Ext. W-l to W-6 on the side of the Union and Ext- 
M-l on the side of the management ns there was no objection 
from either Side for marking these' documents in evidence. 

5. The claim Of the union is for designating Sri Mohan 
Kumar as DCCP of Peya v -I branch with effect from 20-1-1990, 
the date on which he was confirmed in the Bank’s service 
and for payment of allowance continuously from that day 
onwards. Admittedly Sri MOhan Kumar was confirmed in the 
Bank's service with effect from 20rl-1990 as peon and 
Sri .Surendran was confirmed with effect from 25-4-1990 
as peon. The management designated Sri Surendran as DCCP 
on the basis of his branch seniority. Accoiding to the union 
temporary employee* and employees on probation should 
not he entrusted with any of the duties attracting special 
allowance. Reliance was placed on Exts. W-l staff circular 
issued by the Deputy General Mannger of the Management 
wherein it is specifically stated so. Ext. W-2 is copy of 
tetter from the -Reaffinal Manager of the Bank to the.,Peyad 
branch Regarding DCCP allowance! to subordinate staff. 


It is specifically stated in Ext. W-2 that on the basis of 
ciicular dated 7-3-1972 of the Bank DCCp..allowance is 
payable only after confirmation and as such Sri Mohan Kumar 
is eligible for the post of DCCP. Ext. W^4 is a letter 
addressed to the manager of Punnayurkulam branch from the 
Regional Manager. It is stated in Ext._W-4 that as per staff 
circular dated 16-3-1991 temporary employees and employees 
on probation should not be entrusted with any of the duties 
attracting special allowance, As per Ext. W-4 the Regional 
Manager has pointed that payment of DCCP allowance to an 
employee on probation in that branch was not in order. 
It was also pointed out that another confirmed employee 
,n that branch alone is eligible for DCCP allowance. Ext, W-l 
W-2 and W-4 make it clear that the practice followed in the 
Bank for the posts eligible for DCCP allowance were given 
only ufter confirmation. The management has not furnished 
any circular to show that the employee on probation was also 
given the post of DCCP. No specific. Instances of such 
posting were' also brought to the notice of this Tribunal 
to support the contention of management in this regard. 
It is also not established by producing any circular or 
pointing out any specific instances that the normal practice 
followed for filling up of special allowance carrying posts 
for subordinate staff is eentrewise/branch seniority. It is 
also not established that seniority is normally counted 
with reference to the date of joining at the branch and not 
on the. date of confirmation. On the other hand Exts. W-l and 
W-2 clearly establish that only confirmed employees as peon 
have been given posting in special allowance carrying post. 
It is true that Sri Surendran joined the Peyad branch on 
25-10-1989 and Sri Mohan Kumar joined there on 27-10-1989 
only. But ns I have held above only confirmed employees are 
eligible for the post attracting special allowance. So ihe 
action of management in denying the post, of DCCP to Sri 
Mohan Kumar is unfair and unjustifiable. The management 
has thus overlooked the claim-Of'Sri Mohan Kumar and given 
the posting , to Sri K. Surendran which is highly, illegal and 
unjust. On the basis of Exts. W-l, W-2 and W-4 “nd on the 
basis of precedents followed in the Bank Sri Mohan Kumar 
alone is eligible for DCCP allowance in Peyad branch With 
effect from 20-li 1990. 

6. In the result, an award is passed holding that the action 
on the part of the management of State Bank of Travancore 
in deying the. post of DCCP to Sri Mohan Kumar at the Peyad 
branch is unfair and unjustifiable. Sri Mohan Kumar 
accordingly entitled to get the posting as DCCP of Peyad 
branch ' continuously With effect from 20-1-1990 and also 
special allowance for the entire period. 

C. N. SASIDHARAN, Presiding Officer

AWARD 

This industrial dispute has been referred for adjudication 
lo this Tribunal by the Government of India as per Order 
No. L-12012/157/91-IR (B-1II) dated 7-6-1991. 

The issue for adjudication is the following : 

“Whether the action on the part of the management 
of Lord Krishna Bank Ltd., in termina ting/denying 
Sri P. Srinivasa Pai employment as. Peon beyond 
12-12-1985 is fair and justifiable ? If not, to 
what relief the said workman is entitled to 7” 

2. In answer lo notices issued from this Tribunal the 
management and union enteTed appearance and filed state¬ 
ments advancing their respective; contentions. Thereafter, 
while the case was pending fo r evidence both sides submitted 
thut there was likelihood of compromise. Accordingly the 
case stood for reporting settlement on several dates and finally 
on 29-4-1993 the union filed a statement stating that in 
pursuance of clause 6 of the memorandum of settlement 
dated 29-1-1993 the union is withdrawing the dispute, A copy 
of the memorandum of settlement was also produced. The 
learned counsel foi the management accepted the copy of the 
statement filed hv the union submitted (hat the- management 
has no objection in, withdrawing the dispulc by .the union 


3. As requested by the union, the settlement dated 
29-1-1993 is accepted and an award is passed in terms of 
the settlement a copy of which is shown as Annexure to 
this award.

Short Recital of the case 

A settlement was signed on 10th April, J992 between the 
management of Uord Krishna and Ltd., and their workmen 
represented by the Lord Krishna Bank Employees’ Union on 
some of the issues /demands raised by the union as per their 
letter dated 21st June 1990. As per clause 4 of the said 
settlement it was agreed to settle all the remaining issues 
and disputes by mutual discussions in cordial atmosphere 
at ail early date, Thereafter mutual/discussions were held 
on various dates and finally an amicable settlement was reached 
in the joint discussions held on 12th October 1992 at Erna¬ 
kulam. Subsequently a separate settlement In respect of daily 
wagers was signed before the Regional Labour Commissioner 
(Central) Cochin, on 25th January 1993. This settlement now 
covers the remaining issues. 

Terms of settlement 

1. Payment of back wages and bonus to daily wagers—It 
is agreed to settle the issue of back w>ages and bonus payable 
to certain daily wagers as under : 

(a) Tt is aereed by the management to pay back wages 
front 30-5-1991 i.e., the date of the award of Indust¬ 
rial Tribunal, Alleppey, in I. D, 96/89 (C) to Mr. 
V. Balnkrlshna Pai and Mr. R. Krishnnkumar. 

<b 1 Regarding the payment of minimum bonus to the 
daily wagers it is aereed to take the period of engage¬ 
ment from 1-1-1989. 

2. Cases filed by Mr. C. N. Narayana Pai—1 l i;, agreed to 
withdraw all the cases filed by Mr. C. N. Naravapa Pai befote 
the (Central Government Labour Court, Ernakulam with imme¬ 
diate efleci. A compensation of Rs. 9,775 will bg paid to

3. Cases filed by Part lime sweepers of Azhiyanmir

Ktlshnanundan and Mr. P. K. Sureshkumar, Part-time sweepers 
of Azhiyannur branch anti the difference of wages of Rs. 1.851) 
and Rs. 1,680 will be paid to them respectively.

5. Appointment on contract basis—Management explained

is constrained to make certain temporary appointments until 
the staff position is improved. Postal for discussion in the

It is further agreed by both the parties that if any dispute

by mutual discussions in a cordial atmosphere.

BFFORE SHRI GANPATI SHARMA . PRESIDING

4. Tho matter was fixed for filing of documents by (he par¬ 
ties when the parties started negotiations and ultimately U was 
settled. The management representative filed on application 
that the amount so deducted has since been refunded to. the

compensation. 

J. I. have heard representatives for .he parties and have 
pone through the record. 

6 . Since the amount deducted ha* already been refunded 
by a mutual settlement between the parties there seems to De 
no ground to allow any compensation in the form of interest 
etc. because that has to be allowed while deciding the dispute 
between the parties and here the matter has been settled and 
the dispute is not being decided on merits. I. therefore hold 
that no dispute eexists between the parties now and a No Dis¬ 
pute award is given in this case, he Management shall pay 
Rs. 250/- as costs of this litigation to the workman.

(71 The Petitioner-Union has aigucd that this decision of 
tire Board of Trustees which has approved the conclusions

(8) The Chairman of the Port Trust has addressed the 
Uuion Government to give approval to the amended irfiaft) 
rule and have it published m the gazette in oilier to legman 
(lie settlement recorded in Ex. W-ll minifies for implementing

examination. Having made Fromotions in this manner from 
19/9 to 1986 Uie Ciiairman has suddenly revised the formula, 
and gave promotions on the- basis ol 1 : 1 ratio with retros¬ 
pective ettect, cancelling all the earlier promotions made on Uie: 
basis oi J : 1 and in ihat process he has either demoted the 
promoiee U.D.Cs or reduced their seniority ranks and theic- 
oy unsettled the promotions made between 1979 and 1986, 
to tnc detriment ot these promoters who came under the senio¬ 
rity quota of 3 : 1 (.75 per cent against 25 per cent). These 
shifts in the factual position in a chronological ordei stated 
hereunder can help easier understanding of the matter in dis¬ 
pute. 

FIRST ST AGE : Before the merger of the minor port of 
Tuticonn and New Port of T uticonn which took place on 
1st April, 1979, the Shipping Ministry of the Central Govern¬ 
ment was in direct control in the absence ol a major port 
trust. Prior to 1st April, 1979 the ratio 3 : 1 was observed 
by the Central Government in tilling the posts of U.D.Cs by 
promoting L.D.Cs. 

SECOND STAGE : Now the merger of the two ports into 
T uticorin New Port coming under the purview of Major Port 
Trusis Act has taken place with a Board of Trustees taking 
over the reins of the major ports’ administration. The Board 
of Trustees is empowered u/s 28 of the* Major Port Tiusts 
Act to make regulations or rules in respect of appointment, 
promotion etc. S. 124 of the same act enacts that any such 
regulations or rules can have 1 effect only after they get approv¬ 
ed by the Shipping Ministry of the Central Government and 
the said approval gets published in the Gazette. While so, the 
board changed the' pie 1st April, 1979 rule giving 3 : 1 ratio 
into J : 1 which has the effect of reducing the quota of 
promotees on the basks ot seniority, in the grade of L.D.Cs. 
This new rule effective from 1st April, 1979 is one' approved 
by the Central Government. 

STAGE HI : However the new approved rule prescribing 
1 : 1 ratio to become operative from 1st April, 1979 was not 
allowed to be implemented by the workman (L.D.C.) vehe¬ 
mently opposed to the ratio. This resulted in negotiations lead¬ 
ing to the settlement u/s, 12(3) Ex. W-8 and another settle¬ 
ment couched in the form of conclusions reached in the 
course of discussion, as recorded in the minutes Ex, W-ll. We 
have already seen that the: board of Trustees has accepted 
Ex, W-ll agreement us true and gave their approval thereto 
and the Chairman wrote to the Central Government seeking 
the Government’s approval u/s. 124 of the Major Port Trust 
Act, vide Ex. M-10 dated 10th March, 1981. 

STAGE IV : In view of the agreement Ex. W-ll the 
Board’s approval and the anticipated approval to be given by 
the Central Government the Chairman filled up the post of 
U.D.C. by adopting 3 : 1 ratio and hereby keeping the 
approved rule providing 1 : 1 ration in abeyence. Thus, 1 : 1 
ratio even though ordained by the approved rule was given 
a go-by even in 1979 because the management of the New 
Port Trust has accepted 3 : 1 ratio for all practical purposes 
and filled up the vacant posts of U.D.Cs by giving 75 per 
cent of the posts to the seniors in the grade of the L.D.Cs. 
This implementation of 3 : 1 ratio by ignoring 1 : 1 ratio, 
has been made continuously upto May, 1986. 

STAGE V and LAST STAGEThe anticipated approval 
by the Central Government, that Is, Shipping Ministry has not 
been given between 1980 and 1986. Uusing this delay, which is 
inordinate, (be Chairman of the Port Trust has deviated from 
the agreed and accepted ratio of 3 : 1 and suddenly honoured 
the approved rule of 1979 which provides 1 : 1 ratio, on the 
plea that the Chairman cannot flou.t an approved rule any 
longer and the Merit quota of 50 per cent (1 : 1) should be 
filled up retrospectively from 1979. This stand of the Chair¬ 
man is reflected in his policy note file Ex. M-15. 

(9) A major lacuna found by the respondent in the agree¬ 
ment or settlement contained in Ex, W-l I is that it fails to 
meet the incredients of a settlement as defined in the I.D. 
Act. The agreement I believe is a direct and logical off shool 
of the earlier Sec, 1213) settlement Ex. W-8. A few other 
demands have been actually discussed in the presence of the 
Conciliation Officer as shown by EDt, M-4. The agreement on 
3 : 1 ratio is the only one item not directly arrived at in the 
Physical presence of the conciliation officer on 24th August, 
1979 vide Ex. W-ll. Bfll K gnbstance, the authority of the 
conciliation officer in very much implied in the entire course 

126S GI/93—10. 


of the discussions and lift vary SQUrCfi or origin of the settle 
iiit’m dated 24th August, 1979 (Vide Ex. W-ll). is ahuiUed 
cy Sec. j 2(3J settlement Ex. W-8. Above all, tne agreement 
on j, : J ratio tins been honoured in practice o> the Chuii- 
n,an ui the Poit Trust for above 8 years without the: apptoval 
ot the Union Government. Failure of the Union Government 
to consider the request made in Ex, M-10 icttei dated 10th 
iviarch, 1981 that the diaft amendments ue uppioveu die 
Ministry of Shipping is a clear case of the evil of red-Up.sm 
arid ictliary. All the relevant facts have been summaris'd in 
Ex. M-5 letter sent by the Union to the Asst. Labour Com¬ 
missioner (Central) Madras. Para-7 ot the Ex. W-5 recites that 
a copy of the agreement dated 24th August, 1979 (Lx. VV-11/ 
was scut to Uie Ministry and the Asst. Labutir Commissioner 
(C) Madras by the Port in its letter No, F-l/4412 79-A dated 
J2th October, 1970. This assertion Dy the Union has not been 
denied by the Respondent, i thereiore, persums and conclude 
ihat copy of the agreement prescribing 3 : 1 ratio by dropping 
1 : 1 nas been sent to the Authorities contemplated in Sec 
tion 2(p) and Section 12(3) of the i.D. Act and therefore 
it deserves to be treated as a valid seUiement capable of en¬ 
forcement even in the absence of formal approval by the Cen¬ 
tral Government under Section 124 of the Major Port Trust 
Act, 1963, In any view of the matter it must be deemded to 
Ve a valid settlement in as much as it has been rightly imple¬ 
mented by the Chairman between 1979 and 1986. Therefore, 
it is not open to the Chairman of the Port Trust to deviate 
from ihe settlement Ex. W-ll taking umbrage under the delay 
in according approval by the Central Government and change 
the pa Kern ot promotion arbitrarily. T he Chairman without 
any direction from the hoard of Trustees cannot review the 
legality of 3 : 1 ratio, brand the promotion on that basis 
as ad hoc and make a volte-face. It is interesting to note 
that the Central Government has finally approved on 29tn 
January, 1987 vide Ex. M-ll notification, the settlement as 
embodied in the draft amendment ro the regulation pres¬ 
cribing 5 . 1 for promotion of L.D.Cs. It is therefore clear 
that the Chairman’s order/orders under which he has given 
retrospective promotion including some prospective promo- 
lions, if any to the L.D.Cs on the basis of merit against 
50 per cent vacancies and thereby divested the seniority- 
based pioniotces, of their promotion and ranks in the grade 
of U.D.Cs are illegal. As a result, it is but necessary to 
cancel all the promotions given by the Chairman on the 
basis of merit against 50 per cent vacancies, namely, 1 : 1 
ratio and protect the interests of U.D.Cs already promot¬ 
ed as such against 75 per cent quota on the basis of senio¬ 
rity ot L.D.Cs. It follows that revised promotion or re¬ 
vised seniority ordered by the Chairman in terms of Ex. 
W-7 and Ex. M-15 giifinyj .more posts to the merit quota is 
contrary to the settlement Ex. W-ll which is line, valid 
and lawful and which cannot be wished away by the res¬ 
pondent or even by the Central Government. 

(10) After giving promotions on the basis of accepted 
3 : 1 ratio between 19-12-80 and 9-4-86, the respondent or 
the Chairman thereof cannot turn the tables arbitrarily. In 
any view, in action on (he part of the Central Government 
till September, 1987 (the dale of Ex. M-ll) does not mean 
negation of Ex, W-ll settlement, or draft amendment to 
the rule, by the Central Government, We cannot read in 
Section 124 ol the Major Port Trust Acl any power con¬ 
ferred on the Central Government to over ride a settlement 
such as Ex. W-ll which has all the attributes of Section 
12(3) settlement. After considering the facts appearing in 
the evidence and the position of law, I find on this point 
that all actions of the Respondent-Chairman relating to pro¬ 
motion of lower division clerks as upper division clerks on 
the basis of the 1 : 1 ratio, reversion of already promoted 
candidates and preparation of revised seniority list of U.D.Cs 
on the basis of 1 : 1 ratio are illegal and liable to be set 
aside. The Respondent has vainly characterised the reference 
or lather the form of the question referred to as factually 
incorrect or meaningless. The question referred to is no 
doubt improperly worded but its meaning is apparent. The 
question pertains only to the aclions of the Chairman as 
reflected in his circular dated 10-4-1986, The point which 
1 have formulated for determination reflects the correct 
meaning of the reference. 

(11) In the result, an award is passed declaring that all 
actions taken by the Respondent and its Chairman with 
regard to promotion of lower division clerks by adhering to 
1 : 1 ratio on the basis of Ex. W-7 circular dated 10-4-1986 
are invalid and that all the promotions effected on the bm 



1898 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 19, 1993/JYAISTHA 29, 1915 [P art H- Sec. 3(ii)j 


of ? . 1 ratio from 1-4-1979 onwards' shall remain valid and 
effective for all purposes and that the ratio 3:1 shall be 
implemented until it is lawfully changed by a fresh settle¬ 
ment. No costs.

man, Tuticorln Port Trust to Thiru G. Thangapandy, 
Overseer (Copy).

AWARD 

The Central Government by their letter dated 25th 
April, 1990 addressed to this Tribunal made the 
reference to the effect : 

“Whether the demands contained in letter No. 
TD|31 ]2614|89 dated 09-10-1989, addres¬ 
sed by the Transport & Dock Workers’ 
Union to M|s. Jivika Agencies, 299, Narshi 
Natha Street, Masjid Bridge, Bombay- 
400009 (copy enclosed as Annexure I) are 
justified ? If so, what relie the workmen 
concerned (Names given in Annexure II) 
are entitled to ?”. 

The Workers’ Union filed the statement of claim 
and the Jivika Agency filed its Written Statement. 

My learned Predecessor framed Issues on 08-11-1990 
as under : 

(1) Has this Tribunal jurisdiction to proceed 
with the adjudication for the reason that 

(i) Labour Court is the only proper forum to 

entertain the matter and | or 

(ii) that the Central Government is not the 

appropriate Government ? 

(2) Is the retrenchment of the 13 workmen de¬ 
tailed in Annexure II to the order of refer¬ 
ence illegal and unjustified ? 

(3) If the answer to Issue No. 2 is in the affir¬ 
mative, what reliefs are the wor km en en¬ 
titled to, regarding reinstatement and back- 
wages ? 

(4) Is the settlement dated 26-06-1989 binding 
on the Management ? 

(5) Relief and costs ? 


Meanwhile a settlement signed by the Assistant 
Secretary of the Transport and Dock Workers’ Union 
and the Constituted Attorney of M|s. Jivika Agencies, 
dated 8th February, 1993 has been filed. 

The Advocates of both the parties have also 
affixed their signatures to the said settlement. As a 
result of the said settlement, reached between ffie 
parties, it is not necessary to answer any of the Issues. 


The said settlement is favourable to both the parties 
and they recommend the acceptance of the same, and 
1 am also assured by their learned advocates. Under 
the circumstances, the Award in terms of the settle¬ 
ment. No order as to costs.

ANNEXURE 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUS 
TRIAL TRIBUNAL, NO, 1, AT BOMBAY 

PRESENT : 

Sliri Justice R. G. Singhakar, Presiding Officer. 

Reference No. CGIT— l J65 of 1992 
PARTIES : 

The Employers in relation to the Management 
of Hindustan Petroleum Corporation 
I imiled. 

AND 

Their Workmen 
APPEARANCES : 

For the Employer.—Sliri Pota, Advocate. 

For the Workmen.—No appearance. 

INDUSTRY : Petroleum Industry STATE : Goa 
Bcjmbay, the 13th May, 1993 

AWARD 

The Government of India, Ministry of Labour, by 
their letter dated 25th September 1992 has made the 
reference to this Tribunal. The point is; 

“Whether the action of the Management of 
Hindustan Petroleum Corporation Limited. 
Vasco Terminal, Vasco Du Gama—Goa In 
introducing one shift from 7.00 a.m to 
3,00 p.m. in place of two shifts i.e. from 
7.00 a.m. to 3.00 p.m. and from 3.00 p.m. 
to 11.00 p.m. for the vehicle drivers with¬ 
out issuing notice under Section 9-A of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 read with 
Rule 34 of the Industrial Disputes (Central) 
Rules, 1957 is legal and justified? If not, 
what relief can be granted to the work¬ 
men 

This Was received by this Tribunal in October, 1992 
and notices were issued to the parties to file state¬ 
ment of claim and written statement. The notice 
was duly served on the Workmen’s Union, but there 
was no appearance on behalf of the Union, while the 
management was represented by die learned advocate 
Mr. Pota. He continued to appear on behalf of the 
management and there was no appearance on behalf 
of the Union. Another notice was served on the 
Union and yet there was no appearance on 12th 
May, 1993 to which date file matter was adjourned 
and also no statement of claim was filed on behalf 
of the Workmen’s Union enabling this Tribunal to 
know the nature of the dispute between the parties. 
1208 GI/93—11 


As a matter of fact, it is obligatory on the part 
of the Union under the Rules Framed under the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 to' file the statement 
of claim. Relevant Rule is Rule 10-B. Not only 
statement of claim cojmplctc to be filed, but with 
relevant documents, list of reliance and witnesses has 
to be filed. This has not been done and therefore 
it is not possible for this Tribunal, particularly in 
the absence of any representative of the workmen 
being available before this Tribunal for questioning'. 
The obvious answer to the reference is that it has 
not been sure whether the action of the Manage¬ 
ment of Hindustan Petroleum Corporation Limited, 
Vasco Terminal, Vasco Da Gama—Goa in introduc¬ 
ing one shift from 7.00 a.m. to 3.00 p.m. in place 
of two shifts, i.e. from 7.00 a.m. to 3.00 p.m. and 
Irom 3.00 p.m. to 11.00 p.m. for the vehicle drivers 
without giving nolice under Section 9A of the Indus¬ 
trial Disputes Act, 1947 read with Rule 34 of the 
Industrial Disputes (Central) Rules, 1937 was illegal 
and unjustified, that being the position. 

Award accordingly. 

R. G. STNGHAKAR, Presiding Officer.

APPEARANCES :

STATE : Bihar INDUSTRY ■ Airlines 

Dated, Dhanbad, the 17lh May, ,1993 

AWARD 

The Government of India, Ministry of Labour in 
exercise of the powers conferred on them under Section 
10(1) (d) of the 1. D. Act, 1947 has referred the 
following disputes to this Tribunal for adjudication 
vide their orders referred to above. 

THE SCHEDULE OF REF. NO. 12 OF 1990 

“Whether the action of the management of 
Airlines, Patna in terminating the services 
of Shri Mahesh Prasad Gupta, ex-Casual 


Loader w.c.f. 2S-3-89 is justified. If not, 
what relief is the workman entitled to ?” 

THE SCHEDULE OF REFERENCE NO. 17 OF 

1990 

“Whether the action of the management of 
Indian Airlines, Patna in terminating the 
services of Shri Chandrika Prasad, Casual 
Loader w.c.f, 4-5-89 is justified. If not, what 
relief is the workman entitled to?’’ 

THE SCHEDULE OF REFERENCE NO. 31 OF 

1991 

"Whether the action of the management of Indian 
Airlines, Puma in terminating the services of 
Shri Naresh Kumar, Casual Loader w.e.f. 
1-3-87 is justified. If not, what relief is he 
entitled to ?’’ 

2. All the three references named above were heard 
analogous and are disposed olf by a common award. 
It was the common case of all the three concerned 
workmen that they were under the employment of the 
management of Indian Airlines as casual labour. They 
worked for several years completing more than 249 
days in a calendar year. But suddenly they were stop¬ 
ped without any notice or notice pay. They were 
appointed orally with the assurance that appointment 
letters will be given in due course but lastly no such 
letters were issued. The management issued tempora.y 
permit in proof of employment but did not regularise 
the services inspite of line Tact that the concerned 
workmen rendered the services for years together. Die 
management while terminating the services of the 
concerned workmen did not follow the procedure laid 
down under Section 25G of the 1. D. Aclt and the 
services of the junior hands namely Jagdevan Singh, 
Rabindra Mandal, Rajendra Kumar and others were 
retained. The management also failed to follow the rule 
77 of the Industrial Disputes (Central) Rules, 1957 
under which it was obligatory on the part of the 
management to maintain seniority list of the workmen 
in respect of category and to display the same on the 
notice board for employment of retrenched workmen. 
Die stoppage of the work of the concerned workmen 
is retrenchment which is illegal, bad in law for the 
management failed to issue one months notice, notice 
pay and retrenchment compensation which are con¬ 
dition precedent to be followed under the provision 
of Section 25F of the ].D. Act. The workmen are 
thus entitled for their reinstatement with full back 
wages. 

3. The management filed W.S, seperately in all three 
references and the common grounds taken are as 
follows 

[t was stated that the alleged dispute is not an 
industrial dispute within the meaning of Section 2(k) 



iqp?l : ®rfT 19, 199:1/^4*3 29, 19 15 


1903 


[srW Il-tiz 3 (ii)] 

of the I. D. Act. The casual workmen got work tern- 
porarily in absence of the regular employees and they 
do not have any guaranteed right of employment. The 
names of the casual workmen arc not entered in the 
muster roll. 

The employers corporation operates flight to and 
from Patna Airport but it is not possible to adhere 
strict time schedule for its operation depends upon so 
many factor diversed to nature. At several times the 
variation takes place in arrival and departure of the 
aircrafts due to faulty weather and temperature. It 
affects the total activity of work at the airport. The 
work load increases in case of clashing of flights. 

Loading!off loading and cleaning of aircraft is the 
job of regular nature but it looses its character as 
such due to aforesaid various compelling circumstances. 
The corporation has got permanent manpower who 
are in the roll for the purpose of loading!ofli loading 
and also for cleaning aircraft but it has to be engaged 
part time|daily rated|casual^bucllies for the reasons 
stated above and also on account of leave replacement. 
Such engagements are made for specified period which 
is also within the knowledge of the persons so engaged. 
Thus the concerned workmen were engaged intermit¬ 
tently depending upon the requirement of the work 
which were of specific nature and for specific period. 
Therefore the concerned workmen cannot claim em¬ 
ployment as a matter of right employment. It was also 
stated that no concened workman ever completed 240. 
days attendance in any preceding 12 calendar months. 

It was stated that the employer corporation is a 
statutory corporation established under Section 3 of 
the Air Corporation Act, 1953 (being Act No. 27 of 
1953). Under Section 7 of the Act the principal 
function of the corporation has been noted which 
amongst others is to provide safe, efficient, adequate, 
economically and properly coordinated air transport 
services to the best advantage of the people. Again 
Section 45 (clause-2) (b) provides that the employers 
corporation may make legulaiion relating to the terms 
and condition of the officers and other employees of 
the corporation. 

The employer corporation after having obtained 
necessary permission from the Central Government 
framed three sets of service regulation. Further as per 
regulation 4, employers framed the Indian Airlines 
Recruitment and Promotion Rules. Rule 19 of the 
recruitment has been set out in para-10 of the W.S. 
giving out the necessary qualification plus the procedure 
for appointment, 

4. Apart from the common grounds taken by the 
concerned workmen their individual cases may be sfated

U. The concerned workman has proved engage¬

stated that they have been manufactured for t' 
purpose of this case. According to the management 
and MW-i the attendance of the casual loaders 
were marked on plain paper loose papers and the 
same were destroyed after vouchers were prepared. 
But this assertion of the management prima facie 
docs not apear to me very convincing. It be noted 
that the concerned workman are working at the air¬ 
port for the yast several years as casual loaders. 
Definitely it is not the affairs of few days. Absenlism 
and increase of work load are permanent features 
of the management which will always require enga¬ 
gement of casual loaders. Actually this goes to 
establish the nature and character of the job. Conti¬ 
nuity of work for jears determine its nature as to 
whether it is permanent or temporary. Definitely the 
work discharged by the casual loaders is of peren¬ 
nial nature. Even the work can be done for years 
together. Then one cannot conceive of Idea that 
the attendance and hours of work of the workman 
will be noted on loose papers only to be misplaced 
or destroyed after preparation of vouchers. It 
sounds something as unnatuial and unbelivable. Ext. 
W-b series on the other hard show the engagement 
of concerned workman almost everyday in the rele¬

registers bear the signature of Shri R. Prasad the

the Station Manager duiing the relevant period. 
The signature of some airport authority appears at 
certain"places under Ext. W-b According to the wit¬ 
ness it was signed by -Shri Prasad Station Manager. 

I find that the management did not challenge this 
fact even by saggestion to the witness that the signa¬ 
ture did not belong to Shri Prasad. I have care¬ 
fully petsued the signature of Shri R. Prasad appear¬ 
ing on his deposition in Ref. 12)90 and 17)90 and 
1 compared the same with the naked eyes the signa¬ 
ture under Ext, W-6. 1 found that both the signa¬ 
tures were of the one and tho same man. In the 
circumstances I am constrained to hold that these 
concerned workmen have been working as casual 
loaders almost regularly during the year 1985, 1986 


and 1987 and they all seemed to have completed 
240 days in a calendar year. In cross-examination 
it was suggested to the witness that the attendance 
sheet prepared by him (Ext. W-5) series and the 
attendance shown under Ext. W-6 series do 
not tally. However, the suggestion has been denied. 
For example I have counted the attendance of Shri 
Chandrika Prasad for the month of February 1986 
as noted in Ext. W-6 which comes to 87 days. Thei 
same days of attendance has been noted under Ext. 
W-5. Anyway I have already disbelieved Ext. W-5 
series for it was one sided affair without any signa¬ 
ture of airport authority.

be said that there was any violation of Section 25G 
of the I. D. Act.
